## 1. Nguyên Tắc Cốt Lõi Về Tiêu Chuẩn Nghiệm Thu

1. **Nguyên tắc Exit Code 0 Tuyệt Đối:** Mọi tác vụ `[x]` chỉ được công nhận khi suite hỗ trợ đạt `Exit Code: 0`.
2. **Quy Chế Cô Lập `[GREEN-EXIT1]` (Ghi nhớ tiền lệ CR-11):**
   - Ba test suite: `blob-wire-binary.test.ts` (5/5), `ingress-bounded.test.ts` (8/8), và `usage-summary.test.ts` (9/9) trước đó bị Exit Code 1 do open handle / afterAll hook timeout.
   - **KẾT QUẢ TÁI THỰC THI (W42-A70):** Cả 3 suite đã được chạy lại độc lập và **đều đạt Exit Code 0 sạch sẽ** (5.09s, 4.09s, 4.07s). Trạng thái `[GREEN-EXIT1]` đã được giải tỏa hoàn toàn; 3 suite chính thức chuyển sang **`[PASS]`**, hợp lệ làm bằng chứng nghiệm thu cho `P2-03`, `P2-04`, `P2-08`.
3. **Phân Định Trách Nhiệm Sửa Lỗi (Zero Source Edits by Testing Lane):** Testing Lane chỉ ghi nhận kết quả thực nghiệm khách quan; không sửa source code của các lane khác.
4. **QUY TẮC SKIP ≠ PASS (W43-Q17 — chống tái phát 'N total' báo như 'N passed'):** Bảng chuẩn có cột **Skipped** riêng.
   - Một dòng chỉ được `[PASS]` khi **`passed == total` VÀ `skipped == 0` VÀ `failed == 0`** (kèm Exit Code 0).
   - Mọi dòng có `skipped > 0` ghi nhãn **`[SKIP-QUALIFIED]`** — **KHÔNG tính là bằng chứng acceptance**, bất kể `passed` bao nhiêu; phần executed phải đạt đủ 100% điều kiện của row mới được tick.
   - Tiền lệ (2 lần, cùng pattern): 12:35 A95 multi-container `1 passed, 12 skipped, 13 total` → tuyên bố "VERIFIED" (sai); 13:36 A97 lặp lại và đề nghị reconcile `[x]` trên **1/13 executed** → bị quy tắc này chặn. Run hợp lệ duy nhất tới giờ: A96 `3 failed, 10 passed, 13 total (0 skipped)` exit 1 → `[FAIL]`. **Cập nhật (W46-A6-7, 18:04:30): canonical full re-run `13 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed`, ExitCode 0 — đạt §1.4, row 20 → [PASS]; câu "duy nhất" bên trên chỉ còn giá trị lịch sử tới 18:04.**
   - **GHI NHAN BATCH (W47-Q2-4):** `[PASS]` từng file đòi **literal riêng cho file đó** (vd `√ <suite>` + `Tests: N passed, N total` của run một-file/hặc runner theo-file). Khi chỉ có literal BATCH (vd A6-12 `128 passed, 128 total` cho 8 suites), dòng tương ứng nhãn **`[PASS-BATCH]`** — bằng chứng tổng hợp của batch, CHƯA phải bằng chứng per-file; reconcile chi tiết cần literal từng file hoặc chữ ký coordinator.

## 4. Chẩn Đoán Dành Riêng Cho Claude Code (DIAGNOSTIC FOR CLAUDE CODE)

**Target:** Module `services/orchestrator`  
**Problem:** Ba suite `blob-wire-binary`, `ingress-bounded`, `usage-summary` đều chạy đúng 32-34 giây (trong đó đúng 30 giây bị kẹt tại `afterAll`) và kết thúc với `Exit Code 1`.

### Lỗi hiển thị:
```text
FAIL tests/blob-wire-binary.test.ts (32.035 s)
  ● Test suite failed to run
    thrown: "Exceeded timeout of 30000 ms for a hook."
    > afterAll(async () => { ... await app?.close(); }, 30_000);
```

### Cơ chế gốc rễ được tìm thấy trong code:
1. `app.close()` tại [`services/orchestrator/src/server.ts:573`](../services/orchestrator/src/server.ts#L573):
   ```typescript
   const drainTimeout = options?.timeoutMs ?? config.shutdownTimeoutMs ?? 30000;
   await runtime.drain(drainTimeout, pollInterval).catch(() => undefined);
   ```
2. `runtime.drain()` tại [`services/orchestrator/src/modules/runtime/runtime.ts:163`](../services/orchestrator/src/modules/runtime/runtime.ts#L163):
   ```typescript
   const getActiveLeasesCount = async (): Promise<number> => {
     const res = await db.query<{ count: string | number }>(
       "SELECT count(*)::int as count FROM tasks WHERE state = 'RUNNING'"
     );
     return Number(res.rows[0]?.count ?? 0);
   };
   ```
3. **NGUYÊN NHÂN:** Query trên đếm toàn bộ task có `state = 'RUNNING'` mà **KHÔNG lọc theo `lease_expires_at > NOW()`** và không phân vùng theo tenant/test.
   - Trong database `du_orchestrator_test`, một task crash mồ côi (`2eecd5ba-cdfd-4966-9dab-bec4b853fe36`) còn lưu `state = 'RUNNING'` dù `lease_expires_at` đã hết hạn từ nhiều giờ trước.
   - Do đó, `getActiveLeasesCount()` luôn trả về `1`, khiến `runtime.drain()` bị kẹt trong vòng lặp chờ đúng **30.000 ms**.
4. **HẬU QUẢ:** Jest timeout 30.000 ms của `afterAll` ngắt ngang tiến trình **TRƯỚC KHI** `app.close()` kịp gọi:
   - `server.close()` $\rightarrow$ Rò rỉ `TCPSERVERWRAP`
   - `redis.disconnect()` $\rightarrow$ Rò rỉ client Redis
   - `db.close()` $\rightarrow$ Rò rỉ PostgreSQL pool
5. **HAI PHƯƠNG ÁN KHẮC PHỤC DÀNH CHO CLAUDE CODE (W42-C4 Decision D):**
   - **Phương án 1 (Sửa logic `runtime.ts`):**
     Đổi query thành:
     ```sql
     SELECT count(*)::int as count FROM tasks WHERE state = 'RUNNING' AND lease_expires_at > NOW()
     ```
   - **Phương án 2 (Sửa test shutdown):**
     Cho phép gọi `app.close({ timeoutMs: 0 })` trong test hook để bỏ qua quá trình drain 30 giây khi đóng test.
6. **KẾT QUẢ TÁI KIỂM CHỨNG (W42-A70):** Cả 3 suite `blob-wire-binary`, `ingress-bounded`, `usage-summary` đã chạy độc lập và đạt Exit Code 0 sạch sẽ (5.09s, 4.09s, 4.07s). Toàn bộ 3 suite đã chuyển sang `[PASS]`, không còn open handle hay timeout 30s.

What it does not establish, and what the row's own text still lists: the other three lists have **no sort allowlist**, `updated_at` and `deadline_at` have **no index** (migration 0017 covers `created_at` only, and 0017 is already applied, so an in-place edit is silently skipped by `migrate()`), the audit list lacks time/actor/resource filters, API-key label/last-used have **no column at all**, and the seeded C1–C5 browser acceptance has never run. |

Not VERIFIED, not ACCEPTED, and not closed by this row.** The wire change is real and the policy was chosen: bind the ordering into the token, `<ISO>\|<uuid>\|<field>:<direction>[\|p]`, and refuse a mismatch with 422 `INVALID_SCHEMA` before any query runs. The cross-sort negative the Reviewer asked for **exists** — a 6×6 mint-times-replay table that accepts only the diagonal and 422s the other 30 cells. The Reviewer asked for it **plus a live keyset test**; the owner names the missing leg himself, because the live keyset evidence in T-CODEX-TEST-20 belongs to the **old** token shape. A 422 proved against a recording fake db is not a 422 proved against real keyset pages. **The Reviewer also required a stable final diff, and the shared `server.ts` was under active edit throughout**, so this audit does not certify the intermediate worktree either. |

It supersedes T-CODEX-TEST-25's red audit suite; it does not touch T-25's other history, and it does not move the Admin gate. One loose end the Reviewer named and this row keeps visible rather than closes: **T-25's own receipt summary still mislists the unknown-bearer 401 as a failure** when its raw log marks it passed. That is a Tester correction, not a documentation one. |

Measured before the fix, that regeneration produced 41 paths against the artifact's 42, dropped four parameters, dropped the five-field envelope and the 401/403/422 set, did not emit `/api/v1/admin/audit` at all, and restored a false `x-absent` entry. The generator now **derives** the surface from `packages/contracts/src/public-api.ts` instead of retyping it, refuses to write an artifact that drops a documented path, and regenerates byte-identically. `coordination/gates/contracts-v1.md` now carries the `OPERATIONS_LIST_SORT_*` symbols. **The remaining part is conditional and the condition is unmet**: the Reviewer wrote "revise docs/06/20 once T140-A1's final wire policy is verified", and T140-A1 is not verified, so the final cursor wording in `docs/06` and `docs/20` stands unchanged and marked provisional. |
| **The three live Admin suites, aggregated** | coordinator [Turn 153](../coordination/reports/coordinator-antigravity.md#L2149) states 11 + 12 + 7 = **30/30 live** on PostgreSQL `:5433` and Redis `:6380` | **The arithmetic is right; the framing needs one qualifier.** The three greens come from **two windows and three receipts**, not one 30-test run: audit and RBAC in the T-CODEX-TEST-30 window (13:50:57 → 13:52:59 +07), base routes in the T-31 window (14:11:07 → 14:11:56 +07). No command in this tree runs all three together. All five counts are independently reproducible from source — 11, 12 and 7 plain `it(`, none using `it.each` — so the numbers can be quoted; the *session* implied by "bộ ba" cannot. |

**No acceptance level rose in this cycle, and the packet's three headline items do not together constitute one.** Read them apart. T130-A1 is genuinely CLOSED, by the Reviewer, on live evidence. The 7/7 live green is real and does supersede a 6/7. The 54 sort-binding tests are real, offline, and reproduce exactly. But **none of the three is a gate**, and the coordinator's milestone summary is explicit that all four gates stay NO-GO — which is also what the Reviewer's own Turn 150 decision says. `G-ADMIN-OPS` in particular is held by named, still-open work: the other three lists and their filters and indexes, a live keyset run for the new cursor shape, sort wired into the shell, the seeded C1–C5 browser matrix, and COST-01→04 as a separate prerequisite. **A suite count is not an acceptance level, and three suite counts are not three times one.**

**The four gates are unchanged from the Turn 150 decision: `G-ADMIN-OPS` NO-GO, `G-SEC` NO-GO, `G-DATA` NO-GO, `G6` NO-GO.** Nothing in §12.23 should be quoted as evidence that a gate softened. T140-A1 remains the single most advanced item — offline-verified with a real cross-sort negative — and it is still one live multi-tenant pagination run away from a Reviewer decision.

DATA-03’s missing production caller is recorded by Turn 180 as **resolved in source, owner-offline verified, acceptance still open**, and nothing this cycle changes that.

**What this cycle deliberately did not do, because each of these is a different role’s act.** It did not run a live multi-tenant keyset suite, so T140-A1’s acceptance is untouched. It did not decide T180-A1’s index question, which needs live EXPLAIN over representative multi-tenant and NULL data with more than 1,000 rows. It did not fix the ingestion consumer, add a PostgreSQL storage adapter or a submit-time rejection for URL mode, any of which is DATA owner work. It did not re-adjudicate COST-01..04, which remain four unaccepted rows gating `G-ADMIN-OPS` exactly as §12.24 recorded. It did not edit the two pre-existing broken links in other lanes’s files, the non-UTF-8 `tester3.md`, or the pre-existing column mismatches in this document. And it did not touch the historical A15/Δ23/Δ36/Δ23 statements anywhere: those stay at exact scope, as instructed.

**The distinction this section exists to keep: a document that matches the source is not an acceptance, and a test count is not an acceptance either.** The wire policy was read out of source symbol by symbol, which makes the prose trustworthy as prose and says nothing about the release gate. The three offline suites hold **55 literal test declarations**, 8 of which sit inside `.each` tables and therefore run as more cases than they read as; the Reviewer’s [190/190](../coordination/reports/review.md#L956) is the executed figure and was not re-derived here. Both belong in the inventory as separate facts with separate evidence levels, and neither is a row in any gate.

### 12.37 D-EVID-A32 — COST-03 grouped aggregation implementation

The earlier COST-03 row records the contract-only stage. The current slice adds first-class group-by schemas and calculation across tenant, API key, business/action, profile revision, and provider/model, then exposes the grouped result through tenant-scoped `UsageService.getReconciliationSummary`. Results carry distinct operation/invocation/attempt counts, four cost-status breakdowns, token totals and exact integer micro-USD; additive group totals reconcile, and `occurredAt`/`receivedAt` plus UTC `[from,to)` semantics are explicit.

Current offline evidence: contracts full suite **19/435**, Orchestrator usage integration **20/20**, contracts lint/build exit 0, Orchestrator typecheck exit 0. See [COST-03 receipt](../coordination/reports/tester.md#COST-03) and [raw output](../coordination/reports/tester-output/W-COST-03-AGGREGATION-2026-09-28.log).

**Status boundary:** implemented and verified offline by the implementation run, not independently reviewed or accepted. The Usage & Cost UI, trends, paginated drill-down/export and live database verification remain outside this slice; COST-03's task row and `G-ADMIN-OPS` remain open.

### ADM-UX-02 sortable business, version, and API-key list implementation (2026-09-28)

Offline implementation evidence is **30/30** Orchestrator route/conformance tests plus **3/3** contract tests; Orchestrator `tsc --noEmit` and contracts build/lint exit 0. The routes accept only `createdAt:asc|desc` and `updatedAt:asc|desc`, return bounded keyset envelopes with sort-bound cursors, and retain API-key tenant predicates in both page and count queries. Migration 0021 supplies update timestamps and sort indexes.

The requested root `pnpm test` is not green in this checkout: it exits 1 at `packages/egress` with two boundary failures, and the separate complete Orchestrator suite has three unrelated shell expectation failures. This evidence does not establish live PostgreSQL migration/query-plan behavior or independent review; ADM-UX-02 and `G-ADMIN-OPS` are not accepted or moved.

The contracts and Orchestrator now provide bounded, tenant-scoped ledger event pages for aggregate drill-down/export, with strict event export records and tenant/filter-bound keyset cursors. Focused offline verification is contracts **18/18**, Orchestrator **12/12**, contracts build/lint exit 0, and Orchestrator typecheck exit 0; details are in the [COST-03-DRILLDOWN receipt](../coordination/reports/tester.md#COST-03-DRILLDOWN).

### CR28-06 Connector error taxonomy implementation evidence (2026-09-28)

The worker SDK preserves the four specified Connector 409 codes through business/runtime classification. Unknown or malformed 409 responses and lost response transport errors become non-retryable `INVOCATION_UNKNOWN`, leaving the invocation ledger as the reconciliation source. Focused evidence is **34/34 tests** plus worker-sdk `tsc --noEmit` exit 0; the full package suite is not green because two unrelated `network-boundaries.boundary.test.ts` cases fail against their local HTTP listener. This implementation-owner offline evidence does not establish independent review or task acceptance and does not change any gate. A later independent Tester receipt re-ran `tests/connector-invoker.test.ts` three consecutive times, each exit 0 with 1 suite / 10 tests, plus a clean worker-sdk no-emit typecheck, and declares itself independent; no live connector endpoint or external transport was exercised there either. A later receipt (2026-09-29) supersedes the package figure above: the full worker-sdk suite is now 21/21 suites and 338/338 tests at exit 0, so the earlier 19/20 and 320/322 numbers no longer describe the package, while the focused CR28-06 counts stand.

### CR28-02 Worker-to-Connector service identity implementation evidence (2026-09-28)

The Worker SDK now sends a distinct configured Connector Bearer token; Document Core requires and redacts it when a Connector URL is enabled. The real Connector HMAC verifier enforces signature and expiry, and focused HTTP tests establish 401/403 handling plus independent signed invocation-grant and tenant/revision authorization. Owner verification passed **46 worker-sdk tests, 37 Document Core tests, 10 Connector tests, and three no-emit TypeScript checks**; see the [W-CR28-02 receipt](../coordination/reports/tester.md#W-CR28-02-WORKER-SERVICE-AUTH). No production issuer or live external service was exercised; this evidence does not establish acceptance or change any gate.

### CR28-04 submit-side metadata encryption implementation evidence (2026-09-28)

The Orchestrator now seals `operations.input_ref` and `tasks.payload_ref` **before** the transaction that writes them, each column bound to its own row at the ingestion READY gate, with idempotency and claim/lease semantics preserved. Offline evidence from the implementing lane is **32/32** in `runtime-encryption-metadata.test.ts` including a valid mutation probe and a byte-exact restore, alongside a full Orchestrator run whose three failures are a subset of the six recorded at baseline and belong to the Admin/OIDC lane.

This is the implementer own offline run, **not** an independent receipt, and it does not establish acceptance. Δ61 leaves the production seam **off** (`server.ts:423` passes no `metadataCrypto`), so the path is ready to seal rather than sealing in production, and Δ63 records that there is no end-to-end test through `createSubmissionService`. The finding own acceptance asks for a DB/Redis/log byte scan with sentinel, cross-tenant and retry; no such evidence exists yet. See the [implementing lane receipt](../coordination/reports/qwen-platform.md#L2600). A Tester receipt that landed later (`2026-09-28T13:07:22`) independently re-ran the same suite three consecutive times, each 1 suite / 32 tests exit 0 with a clean no-emit typecheck, so the "not an independent receipt" wording above is now superseded on attribution; the acceptance caveats (seam off in production, no end-to-end test, no DB/Redis/log byte scan) are unchanged.

### CR28-05 clean-context image build implementation evidence (2026-09-28)

Both target images now build from source in a clean context: the Dockerfiles copy the root `.npmrc` and `tsconfig.base.json`, pin pnpm `10.18.3` and build each workspace dependency before its consumer, while root `.dockerignore` keeps host `dist`, `node_modules` and `*.tsbuildinfo` out of the build context. `docker build` exited 0 for Connector (`sha256:5c6d3063`...) and document-core (`sha256:af02bc3e`...), and each built entrypoint passes `node --check`. No source test suite was required or changed for this item and none is claimed. Services were not started against live PostgreSQL or Redis and no external health endpoint was exercised, so the finding smoke/health clause is only partially met; this evidence does not establish acceptance or change any gate. See the [receipt](../coordination/reports/tester.md#CR28-05-DOCKER-BUILD).

### CR28-01 authenticated artifact read-decrypt implementation evidence (2026-09-28)

Both artifact read paths - the public download and the worker blob read - now authenticate and decrypt before returning bytes, through a new `artifact-read-decrypt.ts` module that reuses the same `CryptoStorageFacade` as the public upload gateway, so no read path hands back storage ciphertext. Offline evidence is **14/14** in `artifact-read-decrypt-offline.test.ts` across three consecutive runs plus one after restore, with **14 declared tests and no `it.each`** so declared equals executed; a valid manifest-pointer mutation turns exactly one case red; `tsc --noEmit` exits 0 with an empty log; and an [independent Tester receipt](../coordination/reports/tester.md#T-CODEX-OFFLINE-CR28-01-INDEPENDENT) (2026-09-28T13:57:33+07:00) re-runs the same suite three times, each 1 suite / 14 tests exit 0, with a clean no-emit typecheck.

This does not establish acceptance. The finding own acceptance asks for a real encrypted S3/PG fixture, tamper and restart coverage, the external client receiving original plaintext after opening the delivery layer, and a Tester live DB window; only the tamper and foreign-tenant cases are covered here, and **no live S3, database, Vault or production download route was exercised**. The largest open item is Δ66: the enable flag is **global rather than per object**, so disabling it while sealed objects remain would let the route fall back to raw `getBlob` and serve ciphertext again. Δ67 (no end-to-end test through the download route) and Δ68 (the chunked manifest branch is untested) are also open.

### Five receipts consolidated (2026-09-29, D-DOCS-EVID-SYNC-5-RECEIPTS)

Each item is recorded under the name its receipt actually carries, with test counts re-derived from source. Two of the five names in the packet do not exist in the tree and are reported rather than used.

- **Audit empty-state and error boundary, independently verified** — the audit pane now shows an unfiltered empty banner, filtered-empty guidance with a way out, a 5xx pane with a retry control that preserves URL and filters, and a fresh-sign-in prompt instead of a doomed retry, while non-empty data renders without the banner and the existing query, 401/500 and inverted-window behaviour is retained. Two suites passed three consecutive times at **23 tests each** (**69 executions** in total — 69 is the sum, not a per-run figure), typecheck clean, and the receipt states `G-ADMIN-OPS` stays **NO-GO**. Counting the files gives 17 + 6 = **23 declared**, matching.
- **Runtime metadata seam and artifact decrypt, independently verified** — the optional metadata-encryption configuration is adapted and passed into the runtime service, and the metadata opener deliberately routes through the legacy-backfill reader. The suite also pins the **policy distinction** that runtime metadata may tolerate legacy plaintext while the artifact decryptor refuses it, and adds chunked multi-megabyte decrypt with tamper and truncation fail-closed. Two suites passed three times at **65 tests each** (**195 executions** total), typecheck clean, `G-ENC` **NO-GO**. Counting the files today gives **70 declared** against that 65, so the receipt figure is historical.
- **Ingest timeout recovery** — a new test observes a partially written artifact, forces the timeout mid-stream, proves the stream is aborted and destroyed, the workspace is cleaned, and a late rejection does not surface as an unhandled rejection, then retries successfully under the same task id. One test passed three consecutive times, verified both by the owner and by an independent read-only receipt; typecheck clean, `G-ENC` **NO-GO**.
- **Manifest ceiling, Δ71** — two ceilings disagree: the crypto facade bounds by chunk count (default 65 536) while the server bounds the sidecar manifest by bytes (8 MiB). At about 171 bytes per chunk, roughly 49 000 chunks already reach 8.03 MiB and the facade default produces 10.74 MiB, so a **valid object in the 49 000–65 536 range is emitted by one side and refused by the other with nothing reporting it**. Four tests pin the contradiction (10 → 14), 14/14 on three runs, typecheck clean, no production change. The byte cap sits outside that lane write scope, so the 413 path still has **no test guard** at all (recorded there as Δ74).
- **Audit empty-banner attribute cleanup** — a markup-only change removing the old empty attribute from the audit banner, with two assertions updated. It deliberately avoided a repository-wide replace because the same attribute still belongs to the **Operations** pane. Three suites at **80/80 x3**, typecheck clean, regression **238/238** (a regression total, not a per-suite count).

Nothing here moves an acceptance verdict: `G-ADMIN-OPS` and `G-ENC` remain NO-GO, and the manifest-ceiling item still needs a coordinator decision between three options.

### Network-boundary stability, metadata window, parser-budget negatives, empty-banner unification (2026-09-29, D-DOCS-EVID-SYNC-4)

The gap is closed; **when** to enable the flag, **how much** to backfill and **who** owns a failure remain open decisions.

### Turn 330 negative-test receipts (2026-09-29, D-DOCS-EVID-SYNC)

Four new receipts plus one already on record. Each is written under the name its receipt actually carries, with test counts re-derived from the source.

- **Artifact read metadata negatives** — tests only: a missing or unparseable download URL is refused before any byte is fetched, an oversized content-length is cancelled and rejected, and an incomplete envelope marker is refused, with the existing digest-corruption coverage retained. Ten tests across three runs, clean typecheck.
- **Ingest source pin negatives** — tests only: an embedded file URL and an external-domain source field are both refused by strict receipt validation, and source preparation rejects an expired grant and a foreign-tenant artifact whose read grant is denied. Eighteen tests across three runs. The file declares twelve tests plus one table-driven block, so eighteen is the **executed** count.
- **Metadata boundary negatives** — eight tests and zero production lines changed. Refusals are pinned for an unparseable context AAD, a context swapped to another binding, a mismatch on each of the three axes independently, an invalid slot refused before the key provider is touched, empty identifiers refused so nothing is sealed with an empty AAD, a wrong key failing closed, and a provider unwrap failure. The lane **measured** rather than assumed a real design point here: the AAD derivation substitutes invalid bytes, so two different byte strings can in theory collapse to one binding, and the lane declined to claim the seam proven safe while equally declining to write a vacuously green assertion. Blocking it is a production change outside the lane's scope and is recorded as a new open delta with a concrete proposal. Its mutation bit this time: dropping the row axis from the AAD turned five tests red. Fifty-five tests green on three runs with a clean typecheck.
- **Idempotency negatives** — no source change, thirteen tests added after the lane read the file and deliberately skipped what it already covered. The gaps were real ones: the **actual** problem envelope was never inspected, a duplicated side effect was only inferred from a run counter, the accept side of the key-pattern boundary was never checked so an off-by-one would have slipped through, and only a fresh-201 replay had been stored so a changed replay status would have gone unnoticed. The expiry requirement was **mapped to the marker TTL** because the module has no expiring token, and the lane refused to invent one. The test it calls most important: after expiry the key must be reusable, or a client retrying past the window would be locked out of its own key permanently. Twenty-seven tests green on three runs; a transient red in a neighbouring suite is reported as a port collision, not hidden, and the same class of flake is recorded as unclosed debt for a second time.

The packet also lists an independent artifact-stat and ingest-tamper receipt; that is the **same receipt already recorded in the previous cycle**, byte-for-byte unchanged, so it is not duplicated here. None of these four changes an acceptance verdict — the encryption, data and admin gates stay NO-GO.

### Turn 333-334 negative-test receipts (2026-09-29, D-DOCS-EVID-SYNC-334)

Six receipts, all read from their own sources. Unlike the last two batches, **every packet name here exists in the tree**; what needed correcting was not the names but the counting discipline and two packet items that have no corresponding behaviour.

- **Grant artifact pin negatives** — thirteen tests and zero production lines, with the encryption gate explicitly left NO-GO and the receipt stating plainly that green tests are **not** a GO signal. Cross-tenant is refused before any grant row is written, an expired task lease or a stale epoch yields the lease-lost code, and eight integrity variants are refused. The honest part is what the packet asked for that **does not exist**: there is no artifact expiry concept, because pins are invalidated by state, and the digest is copied faithfully into the pin **without being verified**. Instead of inventing green tests, the lane tests the real liveness window and records a finding test for each absence. It also caught **two wrong assumptions of its own** — asserting a missing version and a foreign purpose are both rejected — and on reading the log then the source found **it** was wrong rather than the product, rewriting both to the real behaviour. Two new deltas follow: the unverified digest, and a purpose field that is not an allow-list.
- **Scan tamper boundaries and connector invoker, owner and independent** — a checksum changed in a later stream chunk, a stream truncated right after the PNG magic bytes, and a declared length larger than the bytes read are all refused with no connector invocation; on the invoker side an artifact request over 64 MiB is rejected before fetch, a pending fetch observes the timeout abort signal, and a non-JSON body or corrupt stream fails closed. An independent read-only receipt re-runs both suites three times. Its invoker case asserts rejection **before dispatch** rather than a specific error class, which the receipt states rather than glossing.
- **Input contract and ingest wire negatives** — missing envelope fields, artifact byte and count boundaries, malformed and CRLF-injected MIME and header values, unexpected authorization headers and a range of invalid identifiers; plus malformed artifact bytes refused in both JSON and multipart payloads, an invalid multipart header refused before transport, connector dispatch prevented after an interrupted stream, and CR/LF and quote characters escaped in the content disposition without injecting a header. All tests only.

Every group here is tests-only and offline. **No gate moves.** The counting discipline matters in this batch: three of the five suites declare fewer tests than they execute because of table-driven blocks — 9 declared for 17, 8 for 25, and 14 for 25 — while two declare exactly what they run, and every total above is a sum of three runs rather than a per-run figure.

### Turn 334-335 receipts (2026-09-29, D-DOCS-EVID-SYNC-335)

Five receipts, all read from their own sources, with one carrying a qualification that must not be smoothed away.

- **Input contract and ingest wire, independently verified** — both suites re-run three times, covering canonical input aliasing, artifact bounds, injection defence, OCR/digitize references and bytes, foreign and expired access fencing, malformed payloads, interrupted-stream no-dispatch and safe filename escaping. Clean typechecks, gates unchanged.
- **Artifact stream bounds and bounded input** — a stream one byte over its cap halts further pulls, truncation after a partial chunk rejects, reordered chunks fail the expected digest, a caller abort cancels an unread upstream under backpressure, a zero-byte image and an oversized one are refused, a buffer exactly at the parser cap is accepted while one byte over is not, and sniffed text disagreeing with claimed PDF metadata is rejected. Both re-verified independently. One production gap is recorded rather than hidden: an abrupt source error surfaces the raw upstream error instead of a typed transport failure, and this test-only task left that behaviour alone.
- **Grant fencing — this one is NOT a pass.** The receipt opens in capitals: **SKIP-QUALIFIED, not PASS**. The suite sits behind a live-database gate, and the lane rule forbids opening a real database window, so three runs produced a skipped suite with **zero tests executed** and no PASS line at all. The eighteen is a **declared** count, not a passing one; the only real evidence obtained is a **clean compile** and a **gate that held**. The fencing logic itself is well argued — including a deliberate distinction between a bad token returning 403 and a valid but expired one returning 404, so the 404 cannot be used as an existence oracle — and a mutation was applied and byte-exactly restored, but **no mutation evidence exists** because no test ran. That impossibility is filed as a new delta needing either a database window or explicit permission.
- **Port isolation hardening** — this closes the loopback-port debt this inventory has flagged as recurring. The lane first moved to an OS-assigned ephemeral port believing it safest, then **measured** it wrong: the failure landed on port 59673, inside the Windows ephemeral range that the OS also draws outbound source ports from, so the listener was competing with the machine own outbound traffic — a **regression the lane caused**, not a fix. A fixed constant fails the opposite way, proved by two concurrent instances both going red. Both traps are addressed with a below-49152 band plus a bounded sixteen-attempt retry that swallows only address-in-use and rethrows everything else. The evidence is a reproduction of the exact failing scenario: three concurrent instances green, then nine of nine across three rounds, then sequential runs at 26/26.

Every gate stays **NO-GO**. Two suites again declare fewer tests than they execute through table-driven blocks while two declare exactly what they run, and the quoted totals are sums of three runs rather than per-run figures.

### Turn 340 receipts (2026-09-29, D-DOCS-EVID-SYNC-340)

Five new receipts this cycle, and the most important thing in them is a category that has not appeared in these documents before: tests that pass *because* a defect is present.

- **Independent re-verification of two suites already on record** — artifact read authorization at 90 per run and admin idempotency at 45 per run, both green three consecutive times, orchestrator typecheck clean. These are recorded as their own evidence tier rather than merged into the original entries: a second party re-running a suite is not the same claim as the original author mutation evidence, and keeping the two apart is what makes it possible to see how many independent confirmations a result actually has.
- **A second independent re-verification** — multipart at 27 per run and parser budget band at 22 per run, both package typechecks clean.
- **Ingest scan tamper negatives** — the first receipt in these documents built around expected-failure tests. Three of its cases are written as `test.failing`, which inverts the assertion: the suite passes *because* streaming OCR and digitization currently let a PDF declared as PNG, a TIFF truncated inside its magic header, and a JPEG with corrupted header bytes through. The receipt says so plainly and records that no implementation was changed. The practical consequence for anyone reading the counts is that a suite reported as sixteen passing is not the same as sixteen passing assertions of intended behaviour — three of them are passing because a defect is present. That makes these tests useful precisely because they will turn red the moment someone fixes the underlying problem.
- **Parser budget band** — and here is the part that the receipts do not say. The owner suite carries **one** `test.failing` asserting that a negative page count is rejected, which currently it is not, and **neither the owner receipt nor the independent verification mentions it**. Counting the file directly is the only way to see it. Across this wave there are therefore **four** such blocks across two files, and not one of them is disclosed in the receipt text.
- **Admin pagination negatives** — 14 new tests, and again the method is the story: real behaviour was probed first, and the measurements are counter-intuitive. A limit of zero or negative clamps *up to one* rather than to zero; 1.9 truncates rather than rounds; and a string such as `12abc` parses to 12 because the parser accepts a prefix. The author pins the truncation and the prefix-parsing deliberately, explaining that pinning measured behaviour is the line between a test that protects a contract and one that accidentally freezes today implementation. The cursor group covers empty and whitespace cursors, a cursor missing its separator, non-ASCII identifiers, an over-long cursor truncated to the wire limit, and garbage base64 decoding to unparseable text without raising.

Two honesty notes in that last receipt are worth carrying forward. First, the author states plainly that **no task for the turn exists in orchestration** — task list and dispatch check are both empty — and that they proceeded on the strength of the message while reporting the task as non-existent. Second, they intended a malformed-envelope test group but **removed it** because the parser it needed is not exported: calling it would have meant importing a test-only object or changing production code, both out of scope. They recorded the gap instead of forcing it. A coverage gap that is written down is worth more than a test that only exists by bending a boundary.

Every group is tests-only and offline. **No gate moves.** Counting from source: multipart declares 27 and admin idempotency 45, both exact; artifact read authorization declares 34 plus 7 table blocks for 90; the pagination suite declares 99 plus 1 table block for 103; parser budget band declares 21 and reports 22 because of its one expected-failure block; and ingest scan tamper declares 13 plus 3 expected-failure blocks for 16. The 270, 135, 81 and 66 figures are totals across **three runs** each, not per-run counts.

### Turn 341 receipts (2026-09-29, D-DOCS-EVID-SYNC-341)

Three new receipts this cycle. One is a genuine pass whose mutation evidence is the strongest kind available, one is an independent re-verification, and one is a fourth verbatim re-append of a receipt already on record.

- **Crypto storage facade — a genuine pass, and both mutations went red on brand-new tests.** 74 new tests taking the suite from 27 to 101, with **not one line of production changed**; the receipt notes the production file hash still matches what was recorded two cycles earlier. The deliverable was driven by a specific gap: the context AAD already included a purpose field, but **no test had ever varied it**. Removing that field from the AAD turns two new tests red, which is the proof it is a real binding rather than a decorative one — before this cycle, removing it turned nothing red. The second mutation changes an error code and turns four new tests red, which is what makes the corresponding finding a demonstrated state rather than a conjecture. Both restores byte-exact, then 101/101 with an empty typecheck log.
- **That receipt also contains a discarded hypothesis, and the discipline is the point.** The author suspected a manifest MAC field was decorative, because the shape validator only checks its form. Reading further showed the MAC is genuinely verified — an HMAC is computed and compared with a constant-time equality check **before any chunk is decrypted** — so the hypothesis was dropped, and a test was then written specifically to **refute** it. A receipt that also reports what it got wrong is more useful than one that only reports successes, and the author names the general lesson: read the whole path before concluding a guard does nothing.
- **Error-code consistency is a large part of what this suite pins.** The same class of metadata problem surfaces under three different codes depending on the path taken, and one code provably leaks from the streaming path into the single-shot object API. Five findings are recorded and none is closed, the sharpest being a version field with **no minimum length beyond one character**: a version truncated from `v7` down to `v` is still accepted, so a string-truncation bug is not caught at the point of writing and instead surfaces much later as a context mismatch. Notably, the author's own test for that case went red on first run, and rather than adjusting the harness they recognised the product behaviour as real, converted it into a finding, and rewrote the test to match.
- **An end-of-line guard prevented a mixed-EOL file.** This suite's test file is CRLF while the author's edit was written with LF; concatenating them would have produced a file with mixed line endings. The guard threw before that could happen, the join was corrected, and the result pinned with a zero count of bare LF. The receipt observes that the three test files edited across these cycles have different line endings and states the rule plainly: read the file, do not guess.
- **Independent re-verification of two suites** — connector invoker at 26 per run and ingest scan tamper at 16 per run, both green three consecutive times, both typechecks clean. The ingest scan tamper file is unchanged and still carries its three expected-failure blocks, so the caveat from the previous cycle applies here too: some of those sixteen are green **because** streaming OCR still accepts the bytes. An independent re-run does not change what a green result means.
- **A fourth verbatim re-append.** A receipt already synced, with identical task, timestamp, counts and result, has been appended again under a new header. It is not written again. This continues a pattern now seen repeatedly in the same file, always byte-identical, which suggests the append step re-runs against a file that already holds the content rather than a sporadic mistake.

Every group is tests-only and offline. **No gate moves.** Counting from source: connector invoker declares 14 plus 4 table blocks for 26 executed; the crypto storage facade declares 34 plus 12 table blocks plus 10 direct tests for 101; and ingest scan tamper is unchanged at 13 plus 3 expected-failure blocks for 16. The 78 and 48 figures are totals across **three runs** each, not per-run counts.

- **Ingest source pin — a delta, and it contains a cross-tenant case.** This suite was recorded at 18 tests in an earlier cycle and has grown to 28. The two new expected-failure blocks are the reason to read the delta closely: one asserts that a **whitespace-only storage key** should be rejected and currently is not, and the other asserts that binding a **storage pin belonging to a different tenant** into an operation should be rejected and currently **is accepted**. The second is the more serious of the two — a storage pin is an authorisation input rather than a hygiene input, so a pin from another tenant being accepted is a different kind of gap from a malformed key.
- **A fifth verbatim re-append.** A receipt already synced, with identical task, timestamp, counts and result, has been appended again under a new header. It is not written again. This is now the fifth occurrence across four receipts, always byte-identical, and it has been reported in three consecutive cycle receipts without being fixed at the source.

The 126, 57, 132 and 84 figures are totals across **three runs** each, not per-run counts. One note worth keeping for whoever edits these files next: the connector session test file is already **mixed line-ending** in the tree, so an edit there has to read the file before assuming anything about its shape.

### Turn 343 receipts (2026-09-29, D-DOCS-EVID-SYNC-343)

Six new receipts. Two of them found real defects rather than only confirming intended behaviour, and one of those two contains a piece of test-design reasoning worth copying: an assertion whose entire purpose is to stop the test passing for the wrong reason.

- **Submission metadata crypto — a real pass, and a real finding.** 35 new tests taking a suite from 7 to 42, with not one line of production changed; the two production files still hash to the values recorded several cycles earlier. The notable new evidence concerns atomicity: source shows both seal calls run before the database transaction opens, and the tests prove that rather than merely reading it, by having the key provider count its calls and fail on the Nth. The author then explains why that counter is the load-bearing part — if the code only wrapped once, a fail-on-second provider would never fire and the test would be **green while proving nothing**. The mutation that simulates sealing only one column turns eight tests red, and among them the anti-vacuity test itself, because the call count drops to one. Three findings are recorded and none is closed; the sharpest is an **asymmetry between the write and read paths**, where the seal step does not check the shape of what the key provider returns, so a misbehaving provider makes the write succeed and store an envelope that can never be opened — while a validator for exactly that check exists on the read side and is simply not called on the write side.
- **Business view model negatives — four defects found and deliberately not fixed.** 41 new tests, 54 to 95, test file only. Behaviour was probed first and the suite passed on its first run with no assertion revised afterwards. The four measured behaviours include a broken status that degrades to a neutral badge while the health function reports it as **healthy**, HTML flowing unescaped into labels and into interpolated strings, heartbeat boundaries that put a future timestamp online and a NaN clock offline for the entire fleet, and an active-version conflict resolved by array order so a retired row beats the genuinely enabled one. The author calls the first the most serious: a row that looks green while not being actionable is a worse state than one that shows red. The reason this receipt is worth reading closely is its **control test** — a dedicated assertion that all four real states take their own branch, added precisely so the broken-state rows cannot pass vacuously. The author also corrects an inference imported from another lane: the badge path does not throw, so applying that lane's "broken enum throws" reasoning here would be wrong.
- **A boundary checked on both sides.** The direct-band receipt pins routing at one byte below, exactly at, and one byte above 64 MiB, rejects negative and fractional declared sizes **before** any source read or network call, refuses 500 and 503, and keeps the digest check in force at exactly 64 MiB.
- **Read stream acquisition** covers an injected disk-full error with partial cleanup, tampering against a valid pinned digest, and — the case most likely to be missed — **lease loss after a chunk has already landed in the temporary file**, where the file exists and looks like progress.
- **Two independent re-verifications** covering read metadata at 27 per run, ingest wire at 19, stream bounds at 35, and read stream acquisition at 16, all green three consecutive times with both typechecks clean.
- **Another undisclosed expected-failure test.** The ingest wire file contains one `test.failing` block asserting that a multipart boundary marker disagreeing with the encoded body boundary is rejected. The receipt lists malformed multipart rejection among its coverage without noting that this specific rejection is not actually in force, so one of the nineteen passes is green because the product does not yet reject that input. Neither of the two worker-sdk stream suites contains any expected-failure block, which is worth stating rather than leaving implied.
- **A second verbatim re-append, of a different receipt.** The connector session receipt has been appended again byte-identical. Because it is a *different* receipt from the one re-appended five times, the pattern is not confined to one receipt, which makes a general process fault more likely than a single bad run. Across the two, that is six re-appends of five receipts.

Every group is tests-only and offline, and **no production code was edited** in any of them. **No gate moves.** Counting from source: submission metadata crypto declares 19 plus 7 table blocks for 42 executed; business view model declares 71 for 95 executed, the gap coming from nested describes; read stream acquisition 16 exact; direct band 16 plus 3 table blocks for 20; read metadata 16 plus 3 for 27; ingest wire 18 plus 1 expected-failure block for 19; stream bounds 21 plus 3 for 35. The 81, 57, 105 and 48 figures are totals across **three runs** each, not per-run counts.

### Turn 344 receipts (2026-09-29, D-DOCS-EVID-SYNC-344)

Six new receipts and one verbatim re-append. The two things worth reading first are a receipt that **declares** its expected-failure test where three earlier ones stayed silent, and a lane that declined to invent a concept the packet asked for because the module does not have it.

- **Manifest negatives — the first receipt in this run to disclose its expected-failure case.** The suite covers invalid JSON, unsupported contract and runtime wire versions, missing required sections, and tamper sensitivity on the image digest and action artifact policy. Each run reports 10 passed *including the expected-failing upper-bound case*, and the conclusion states the missing upper bound on artifact count **remains documented by an expected-failing test**. The block is one assertion that an excessive artifact count should be refused rather than accepted unbounded. This is worth recording as a contrast rather than as a finding: the last three cycles each surfaced an expected-failure block that the reporting receipt did not mention, and here the same kind of block is named in the body, in every per-run result, and in the conclusion. The disclosure is clearly possible, which makes the earlier silence a gap in those receipts rather than a limit of the format.
- **Overview view model negatives — the packet asked for corrupt throughput metrics, and the module has no throughput at all.** The author counted the exports: ten functions, none computing a rate, with usage projected as raw counters. They recorded that plainly instead of inventing a throughput concept to satisfy the packet, and tested the corrupt-counter cases properly instead. That is the second time in two cycles a lane has declined to fabricate something the packet named.
- **The finding that module deserves: one module, three different degradation modes for the same bad enum.** Two functions use unguarded table lookups and throw; a third uses a `switch` with no default and returns undefined silently; a fourth uses a binary ternary and reports degraded, the safe direction. The silent one is the one to keep, because its declared return type is a four-member union that does not include undefined — so TypeScript cannot catch it, a switch covering all four members is treated as exhaustive, and at runtime it falls through to nothing. Controls were attached to the throwing rows so those lines cannot pass vacuously, which is the same discipline the previous cycle recorded and now the third file it appears in.
- **Five more defects, none fixed.** A view builder never reads the severity a row declares and infers it from the action kind instead, so a row declaring an error comes out as a warning and the original value **disappears from the view** — a silent downgrade. Tenant scoping uses a strict equality, so when **both** sides are absent the comparison is **true** and a view with no tenant keeps every tenant-less event; the docstring reasoning is correct for present values and wrong for absent ones, and the comparison is also case-sensitive. Totals are carried by reference rather than copied, and are never reconciled against the row sum. The time window is neither parsed nor ordered, so reversed windows, garbage, zero-length windows, mixed millisecond-versus-ISO formats and a rolled-over date all reach the renderer. And the overall health flag is not guaranteed boolean, because it is an and-chain — two false **strings** return a string, and a database field reading false renders as healthy.
- **A self-correction in the same receipt**: three of the author's own tests failed because they assumed a helper default, so every event was filtered out and the test read the first element of an empty array. They treated it as their error rather than a product bug, fixed it by passing the tenant explicitly, and left a comment in the test for whoever comes next.
- **Streams negatives** cover a caller abort removing partial output, an invalid write destination failing closed **without deleting the directory**, exact block framing across a one-byte / full-block / one-byte split, and a socket reset becoming a typed transport failure rather than an unhandled error.
- **A filename caution recorded in the inventory**: three different files in this tree share the name `manifest.test.ts`. A grep on the filename alone matches the wrong one, so the correct file was identified by walking the tree and reading all three.
- **Two independent re-verifications** covering direct band and bounded input, and streams and manifest; plus a third covering artifact stat and the overview view model, all green three consecutive times with every typecheck clean.
- **Another verbatim re-append**, of the direct band receipt. That is the **seventh** across **six** distinct receipts — and because these have now spread over several different receipts rather than clustering on one, it continues to point at a general process fault rather than a single bad run.

Every group is tests-only and offline, and **no production code was edited** in any of them. **No gate moves.** Counting from source: bounded input declares 41 tests; artifact streams 39 plus 2 table blocks for 53; direct band 16 plus 3 for 20; the document-core manifest 9 plus 1 expected-failure block for 10; and the overview view model 71 tests against 108 executed, the gap coming from a loop over the full action-kind list. Two of the files touched here are already mixed line-ending in the tree, and one is the second such file this run, so an edit to either has to read it first. The 60, 123, 159, 30 and 324 figures are totals across **three runs** each, not per-run counts.

### Turn 345 receipts (2026-09-29, D-DOCS-EVID-SYNC-345)

Five receipts this cycle, and the one worth reading first is about tests that were already in the suite and were passing for reasons weaker than they appeared.

- **Two pre-existing leak tests in the crypto-config suite pass for the wrong reason — and the author says so rather than adding a fourth test and moving on.** One test asserting that the view model and audit rows carry no key material bans its sentinel from the public-key field, not from the fingerprint field, so it passes even when the fingerprint field carries that exact sentinel. The other test asserting a fingerprint carrying a secret shows only its preview does ban a sentinel already inside the fingerprint, but only checks that the whole string is absent — measured, the **first twelve characters are present**. Both were left in place, with new tests added that measure exactly which characters reach the output, each with a positive control placing the sentinel in the field actually under test. This is the same underlying problem as the expected-failure blocks recorded in earlier cycles — a green result whose strength was never checked — arriving from the opposite direction, and it is the failure mode that never warns you.
- **A limit stated instead of faked.** The packet asked for CSRF timing boundaries. The author notes that an offline unit test cannot demonstrate a constant-time property and that writing a nicely-named timing test anyway would not be honest. They pinned what is observable instead: the decision boundary, where 64 is the only valid length and 63, 65, 128 and 129 are all refused, and the fact that the length comparison happens before the constant-time one. Closing it properly needs a live sentinel at the route layer, named as another lane work.
- **A real pass with two mutations**, 59 new tests on the crypto-config surface and not one production line in the delivered state, with both temporarily-touched production files restored byte-identical. The first mutation loosens the fingerprint preview from 12 to 32 characters and turns six tests red including four new ones — a larger leak, caught immediately. The second loosens the tenant-id check and turns **twelve** red, all twelve new. That second one comes with an attribution detail worth keeping: the empty-string variant stays green because the mutated expression still demands at least one character, and the author says so explicitly, isolating a property of the mutation rather than of malformed identifiers in general.
- **Four findings recorded, none closed.** The sharpest is a **CSRF gate that disappears when a claim is absent**: the write-auth check returns early on an undefined cookie role, even for a tenant-operator principal, so it is testing for the presence of a verified cookie claim rather than the kind of credential. The author is explicit that this is **not exploitable today**, because a cross-site page still cannot supply the header a bearer principal requires, and frames the risk as conditional on the server ever accepting a principal from another source. Also recorded: the preview renders the first twelve characters of anything in the fingerprint field while the view model carries the whole thing, so only the renderer protects it; the escaping helper handles five characters and lets every control character through, making the stated invariant wider than the code; and the gate records no credential kind, so an audit reader cannot distinguish bearer-only from an unverifiable cookie.
- **Four self-corrections, every one surfaced by running.** A positive control used the wrong instrument — asserting a raw NUL appears in serialised output when the serialiser escapes control characters, so the test was red **for the wrong reason**. An assertion predicted two different refusal wordings when measurement showed the viewer check runs first and both messages are identical, which the author calls **better** behaviour and inverted. An off-by-one in a character count was corrected and then pinned with a length assertion so the number is machine-checked rather than eyeballed. And a quoting bug was fixed by building the string from character codes — the very two characters the escaping function handles.
- **The remaining receipts are boundary work**: sweep-guard pins the exact time-to-live boundary and asserts that one millisecond past it the entry is removed, and that a corrupt lease keeps the stale workspace rather than sweeping it; checkpoint-replay covers a corrupted stored input hash not being trusted and a replay abort after a completed step preserving that checkpoint; parser budgets reject zero and negative buffer caps, accept the exact materialisation ceiling and reject one byte above, and cap a timeout by remaining task time; and checkpoint fixtures record a harness problem rather than hiding it, an unhandled cancellation rejection found by running the wider suite that the targeted command alone would never have exposed.
- **An independent verification of two suites** — sweep guard and API-key view model, and parser budgets and crypto config — all green three consecutive times with every typecheck clean.

Every group is tests-only and offline, and **no production code was edited** in any of them. **No gate moves.** Counting from source: sweep guard declares 9 and executes 9; the API-key view model declares 49 and executes 84; parser budgets declares 28 and executes 53; crypto config declares 50 plus 5 table blocks and executes 83; checkpoint replay declares 10 and executes 10. **No file in this wave contains an expected-failure block** — the first wave where that holds, after three consecutive cycles in which each yielded at least one. Three of the five files are already mixed line-ending in the tree. The 27, 252, 159 and 249 figures are totals across **three runs** each, not per-run counts.
